Here's what I found:\n\n## LIBERO Evaluation Script\n\nThe evaluation script is located at `experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py`. \n\n### Basic Usage\n\nTo launch LIBERO evaluations, you run commands like:\n```\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\n--model_family openvla \\\n--pretrained_checkpoint openvla/openvla-7b-finetuned-libero-spatial \\\n--task_suite_name libero_spatial \\\n--center_crop True\n```\n\n### Key Features\n\n- The evaluation script will run 500 trials by default (10 tasks x 50 episodes each)\n- The evaluation script logs results locally. You can also log results in Weights & Biases by setting --use_wandb True and specifying --wandb_project &lt;PROJECT> and --wandb_entity &lt;ENTITY>\n- Setting --center_crop True is important because OpenVLA was fine-tuned with random crop augmentations (a random crop with 90% area in every training sample, so at test time the center 90% crop is taken)\n\nThe script supports evaluation on four different LIBERO task suites: LIBERO-Spatial, LIBERO-Object, LIBERO-Goal, and LIBERO-10 (LIBERO-Long).\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"528f7391-9cae-4a61-8339-2f1551cf2f77","uuid":"b1fc0d27-057f-48a8-9e20-f470d39947e2","timestamp":"2026-05-17T19:10:56.405Z","tool_use_result":{"query":"openvla github LIBERO evaluation script","results":[{"tool_use_id":"srvtoolu_01RcRNtQd68XhYkS9WbMDCdg","content":[{"title":"GitHub - The main code repository is available at https://github.com/openvla-oft/openvla-oft, and the author is moojink (Moo Jin Kim).\n\n## LIBERO Evaluation Code\n\nThe repository includes documentation for fine-tuning/evaluating on the LIBERO simulation benchmark task suites in a file called LIBERO.md. The evaluation code appears to be located in `experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py`, which contains the `GenerateConfig` class used for configuring evaluations.\n\n## Key Components\n\nThe LIBERO evaluation setup includes:\n- Configuration class `GenerateConfig` that specifies parameters like pretrained checkpoint, L1 regression settings, diffusion options, number of input images, proprioceptive state usage, and action normalization keys\n- Utility functions for loading VLA models, processors, action heads, and proprioceptive projectors from `experiments/robot/openvla_utils`\n\n## Model Checkpoints\n\nOpenVLA-OFT achieves state-of-the-art results in LIBERO with 97.1% average success rate across 4 task suites. openvla/openvla: OpenVLA: An open-source vision-language-action model for robotic manipulation. · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/openvla/openvla"},{"title":"moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial · Hugging Face","url":"https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial"},{"title":"moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial-object-goal-10 · Hugging Face","url":"https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial-object-goal-10"},{"title":"README.md · moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-10 at main","url":"https://huggingface.co/moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-10/blob/main/README.md"}]},"Based on the search results, I found information about the OpenVLA-OFT GitHub repository and LIBERO evaluation code:\n\n## Overview\n\nOpenVLA-OFT is a project about \"Fine-Tuning Vision-Language-Action Models: Optimizing Speed and Success\". The main code repository is available at https://github.com/openvla-oft/openvla-oft, and the author is moojink (Moo Jin Kim).\n\n## LIBERO Evaluation Code\n\nThe repository includes documentation for fine-tuning/evaluating on the LIBERO simulation benchmark task suites in a file called LIBERO.md. The evaluation code appears to be located in `experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py`, which contains the `GenerateConfig` class used for configuring evaluations.\n\n## Key Components\n\nThe LIBERO evaluation setup includes:\n- Configuration class `GenerateConfig` that specifies parameters like pretrained checkpoint, L1 regression settings, diffusion options, number of input images, proprioceptive state usage, and action normalization keys\n- Utility functions for loading VLA models, processors, action heads, and proprioceptive projectors from `experiments/robot/openvla_utils`\n\n## Model Checkpoints\n\nOpenVLA-OFT achieves state-of-the-art results in LIBERO with 97.1% average success rate across 4 task suites. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012TNdZWTk8Mayxgo5WkMJFq","type":"tool_result","content":"# LIBERO Evaluation Setup and Instructions\n\n## Environment Setup\n\nStart by creating a conda environment per the project's SETUP.md guidelines. Then clone and install LIBERO:\n\n```bash\ngit clone https://github.com/Lifelong-Robot-Learning/LIBERO.git\npip install -e LIBERO\npip install -r experiments/robot/libero/libero_requirements.txt\n```\n\n## Critical Requirements\n\nThe documentation emphasizes specific package versions: \"Python 3.10.14, PyTorch 2.2.0, and\" a custom transformers fork at version 4.40.1 (moojink/transformers-openvla-oft.git). Testing was performed on an NVIDIA A100GPU, and results may vary on different hardware.\n\n## Running Evaluations\n\nUse this command structure for any of the four task suites (substitute the checkpoint and suite name as needed):\n\n```bash\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial \\\n  --task_suite_name libero_spatial\n```\n\n## Key Configuration Flags\n\n- **`--center_crop True`** is mandatory because \"fine-tuned OpenVLA with random crop augmentations (random crop with 90% area in every training sample)\"\n- Default evaluates 500 trials (10 tasks × 50 episodes)\n- Adjust trials with `--num_trials_per_task`\n- Optional W&B logging via `--use_wandb True` with project/entity specifications"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"528f7391-9cae-4a61-8339-2f1551cf2f77","uuid":"adc0342e-5ecf-44e7-8bb7-323199b0b485","timestamp":"2026-05-17T19:11:13.176Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":279090,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# LIBERO Evaluation Setup and Instructions\n\n## Environment Setup\n\nStart by creating a conda environment per the project's SETUP.md guidelines. Then clone and install LIBERO:\n\n```bash\ngit clone https://github.com/Lifelong-Robot-Learning/LIBERO.git\npip install -e LIBERO\npip install -r experiments/robot/libero/libero_requirements.txt\n```\n\n## Critical Requirements\n\nThe documentation emphasizes specific package versions: \"Python 3.10.14, PyTorch 2.2.0, and\" a custom transformers fork at version 4.40.1 (moojink/transformers-openvla-oft.git). Testing was performed on an NVIDIA A100GPU, and results may vary on different hardware.\n\n## Running Evaluations\n\nUse this command structure for any of the four task suites (substitute the checkpoint and suite name as needed):\n\n```bash\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint moojink/openvla-7b-oft-finetuned-libero-spatial \\\n  --task_suite_name libero_spatial\n```\n\n## Key Configuration Flags\n\n- **`--center_crop True`** is mandatory because \"fine-tuned OpenVLA with random crop augmentations (random crop with 90% area in every training sample)\"\n- Default evaluates 500 trials (10 tasks × 50 episodes)\n- Adjust trials with `--num_trials_per_task`\n- Optional W&B logging via `--use_wandb True` with project/entity specifications","durationMs":6675,"url":"https://github.com/moojink/openvla-oft/blob/main/LIBERO.md"}}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01ApR2c7gRU8t2kHhHrGN184","type":"tool_result","content":"# LIBERO Evaluation Instructions for OpenVLA\n\n## Environment Setup\n\nTo evaluate OpenVLA on LIBERO, you need to:\n\n1. **Install LIBERO**: Clone and install the repository, then add OpenVLA-specific dependencies:\n```\ngit clone https://github.com/Lifelong-Robot-Learning/LIBERO.git\ncd LIBERO && pip install -e .\ncd openvla && pip install -r experiments/robot/libero/libero_requirements.txt\n```\n\n2. **Download datasets** (optional): Modified LIBERO datasets in RLDS format are available via:\n```\ngit clone git@hf.co:datasets/openvla/modified_libero_rlds\n```\n\n## Required Transformers Version\n\nThe documentation emphasizes using **\"Python 3.10.13, PyTorch 2.2.0, transformers 4.40.1, and flash-attn 2.5.5\"** for reproducibility. Results may vary with different package versions.\n\n## Evaluation Script Location & Details\n\nThe eval script is at `experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py`. \n\nKey parameters include:\n- `--model_family`: Set to \"openvla\"\n- `--task_suite_name`: Choose from libero_spatial, libero_object, libero_goal, or libero_10\n- `--center_crop True`: **Critical setting** since the model was fine-tuned with random crop augmentation\n- `--num_trials_per_task`: Customize trial count (default 500)\n\nFour pretrained checkpoints are available on Hugging Face for different LIBERO task suites."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"528f7391-9cae-4a61-8339-2f1551cf2f77","uuid":"79dd7afe-ef9a-4d42-a2b9-1138674fc19d","timestamp":"2026-05-17T19:11:13.972Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":462393,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# LIBERO Evaluation Instructions for OpenVLA\n\n## Environment Setup\n\nTo evaluate OpenVLA on LIBERO, you need to:\n\n1. **Install LIBERO**: Clone and install the repository, then add OpenVLA-specific dependencies:\n```\ngit clone https://github.com/Lifelong-Robot-Learning/LIBERO.git\ncd LIBERO && pip install -e .\ncd openvla && pip install -r experiments/robot/libero/libero_requirements.txt\n```\n\n2. **Download datasets** (optional): Modified LIBERO datasets in RLDS format are available via:\n```\ngit clone git@hf.co:datasets/openvla/modified_libero_rlds\n```\n\n## Required Transformers Version\n\nThe documentation emphasizes using **\"Python 3.10.13, PyTorch 2.2.0, transformers 4.40.1, and flash-attn 2.5.5\"** for reproducibility. Results may vary with different package versions.\n\n## Evaluation Script Location & Details\n\nThe eval script is at `experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py`. \n\nKey parameters include:\n- `--model_family`: Set to \"openvla\"\n- `--task_suite_name`: Choose from libero_spatial, libero_object, libero_goal, or libero_10\n- `--center_crop True`: **Critical setting** since the model was fine-tuned with random crop augmentation\n- `--num_trials_per_task`: Customize trial count (default 500)\n\nFour pretrained checkpoints are available on Hugging Face for different LIBERO task suites.","durationMs":6056,"url":"https://github.com/openvla/openvla"}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EUsmsMQY66wzbZB4AZPd43","type":"tool_result","content":"# LIBERO Evaluation Script Analysis\n\n## Model Family Options\nThe script supports `\"openvla\"` as the model family (referenced in configuration and assertions throughout).\n\n## Key Imports\n```python\nfrom experiments.robot.openvla_utils import get_processor\nfrom experiments.robot.robot_utils import (\n    get_action, get_model, normalize_gripper_action,\n    invert_gripper_action, set_seed_everywhere\n)\n```\n\n## Model Loading\nThe model is loaded via `get_model(cfg)`, which is imported from `robot_utils`. The script validates that the model contains normalization statistics for the task suite: `\"Action un-norm key {cfg.unnorm_key} not found in VLA `norm_stats`!\"`\n\nFor OpenVLA specifically, a Hugging Face processor is loaded using `get_processor(cfg)`.\n\n## Inference Call\nAction generation occurs through:\n```python\naction = get_action(cfg, model, observation, task_description, processor=processor)\n```\n\nThe observation dictionary contains `\"full_image\"` and `\"state\"` (concatenated end-effector position, axis-angle rotation, gripper position).\n\n## Action Post-Processing\n1. Normalize gripper from [0,1] to [-1,+1]: `normalize_gripper_action(action, binarize=True)`\n2. For OpenVLA, invert gripper sign: `invert_gripper_action(action)` (converts dataset convention back to environment convention)\n3. Execute as list: `env.step(action.tolist())`\n\n## Note\nThe actual `get_action()` implementation and model inference logic resides in imported utility modules not shown in this excerpt."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"528f7391-9cae-4a61-8339-2f1551cf2f77","uuid":"62fe5990-12b6-482c-b239-2e7a46fc02cf","timestamp":"2026-05-17T19:12:13.611Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":11936,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# LIBERO Evaluation Script Analysis\n\n## Model Family Options\nThe script supports `\"openvla\"` as the model family (referenced in configuration and assertions throughout).\n\n## Key Imports\n```python\nfrom experiments.robot.openvla_utils import get_processor\nfrom experiments.robot.robot_utils import (\n    get_action, get_model, normalize_gripper_action,\n    invert_gripper_action, set_seed_everywhere\n)\n```\n\n## Model Loading\nThe model is loaded via `get_model(cfg)`, which is imported from `robot_utils`. The script validates that the model contains normalization statistics for the task suite: `\"Action un-norm key {cfg.unnorm_key} not found in VLA `norm_stats`!\"`\n\nFor OpenVLA specifically, a Hugging Face processor is loaded using `get_processor(cfg)`.\n\n## Inference Call\nAction generation occurs through:\n```python\naction = get_action(cfg, model, observation, task_description, processor=processor)\n```\n\nThe observation dictionary contains `\"full_image\"` and `\"state\"` (concatenated end-effector position, axis-angle rotation, gripper position).\n\n## Action Post-Processing\n1. Normalize gripper from [0,1] to [-1,+1]: `normalize_gripper_action(action, binarize=True)`\n2. For OpenVLA, invert gripper sign: `invert_gripper_action(action)` (converts dataset convention back to environment convention)\n3. Execute as list: `env.step(action.tolist())`\n\n## Note\nThe actual `get_action()` implementation and model inference logic resides in imported utility modules not shown in this excerpt.","durationMs":5458,"url":"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openvla/openvla/main/experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py"}}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01BvpyHZVGCEVne21L5cM2vT","type":"tool_result","content":"# OpenVLA Utility Code Summary\n\n## Model Loading (`get_vla`)\n\nThe function registers OpenVLA components with Hugging Face Auto classes, then loads the model using `AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained()` with:\n- Flash Attention 2 enabled\n- BFloat16 precision\n- Optional 8-bit or 4-bit quantization\n- Dataset statistics loaded from `dataset_statistics.json` for action denormalization\n\n## Processor Loading (`get_processor`)\n\nStraightforward retrieval using `AutoProcessor.from_pretrained()` with remote code execution enabled.\n\n## Action Inference (`get_vla_action`)\n\n**Preprocessing:**\n- Converts observation image to PIL RGB format\n- Optionally applies center-crop augmentation (0.9 scale) with resize-back logic\n- Handles tensor conversions between PIL, TensorFlow, and PyTorch formats\n\n**Prompting:**\n- Constructs task-specific prompts depending on model version\n- OpenVLA v0.1: \"A chat between a curious user...\" system prompt format\n- Standard OpenVLA: \"In: What action...\" format\n\n**Inference:**\nThe processor tokenizes the prompt-image pair, then the model's `predict_action()` method generates a 7-dimensional action vector with `do_sample=False` (greedy decoding). The `unnorm_key` parameter denormalizes actions using loaded dataset statistics."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"528f7391-9cae-4a61-8339-2f1551cf2f77","uuid":"9e867ab8-c49f-4aee-ae6f-74ea14319cfb","timestamp":"2026-05-17T19:12:15.134Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":6638,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# OpenVLA Utility Code Summary\n\n## Model Loading (`get_vla`)\n\nThe function registers OpenVLA components with Hugging Face Auto classes, then loads the model using `AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained()` with:\n- Flash Attention 2 enabled\n- BFloat16 precision\n- Optional 8-bit or 4-bit quantization\n- Dataset statistics loaded from `dataset_statistics.json` for action denormalization\n\n## Processor Loading (`get_processor`)\n\nStraightforward retrieval using `AutoProcessor.from_pretrained()` with remote code execution enabled.\n\n## Action Inference (`get_vla_action`)\n\n**Preprocessing:**\n- Converts observation image to PIL RGB format\n- Optionally applies center-crop augmentation (0.9 scale) with resize-back logic\n- Handles tensor conversions between PIL, TensorFlow, and PyTorch formats\n\n**Prompting:**\n- Constructs task-specific prompts depending on model version\n- OpenVLA v0.1: \"A chat between a curious user...\" system prompt format\n- Standard OpenVLA: \"In: What action...\" format\n\n**Inference:**\nThe processor tokenizes the prompt-image pair, then the model's `predict_action()` method generates a 7-dimensional action vector with `do_sample=False` (greedy decoding). The `unnorm_key` parameter denormalizes actions using loaded dataset statistics.","durationMs":4458,"url":"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openvla/openvla/main/experiments/robot/openvla_utils.py"}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XmkqAxyiKa53QPLoDKj3w8","type":"tool_result","content":"# OpenVLA Model Loading and Inference Pipeline\n\n## Model Loading\n\nThe `get_model()` function handles model initialization:\n\n```python\ndef get_model(cfg, wrap_diffusion_policy_for_droid=False):\n    \"\"\"Load model for evaluation.\"\"\"\n    if cfg.model_family == \"openvla\":\n        model = get_vla(cfg)\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(\"Unexpected `model_family` found in config.\")\n    print(f\"Loaded model: {type(model)}\")\n    return model\n```\n\nFor OpenVLA models, it calls `get_vla(cfg)` from the openvla_utils module, which retrieves the model using configuration parameters.\n\n## Action Generation\n\nThe `get_action()` function queries the model:\n\n```python\ndef get_action(cfg, model, obs, task_label, processor=None):\n    \"\"\"Queries the model to get an action.\"\"\"\n    if cfg.model_family == \"openvla\":\n        action = get_vla_action(\n            model, processor, cfg.pretrained_checkpoint, obs, task_label, \n            cfg.unnorm_key, center_crop=cfg.center_crop\n        )\n        assert action.shape == (ACTION_DIM,)\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(\"Unexpected `model_family` found in config.\")\n    return action\n```\n\nThe function passes observation data, task labels, and model configuration to `get_vla_action()`, which returns a 7-dimensional action vector.\n\n## Key Constants\n\n- **ACTION_DIM = 7**: Output dimension for robot actions\n- **Image resize size**: 224×224 pixels for OpenVLA\n- **Device**: GPU (cuda:0) if available, otherwise CPU\n\nThe complete inference relies on external `openvla_utils` implementations not shown in this excerpt."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"528f7391-9cae-4a61-8339-2f1551cf2f77","uuid":"48ddd4a7-23ae-47c0-9ce1-968f50a9cd69","timestamp":"2026-05-17T19:12:31.346Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":3405,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XtEwn3xdgG5caUZHqrD565","type":"tool_result","content":"# LIBERO Evaluation Setup Instructions\n\n## Clone and Install LIBERO\n\n\"Clone and install the [LIBERO repo](https://github.com/Lifelong-Robot-Learning/LIBERO)\" using these commands:\n\n```bash\ngit clone https://github.com/Lifelong-Robot-Learning/LIBERO.git\ncd LIBERO\npip install -e .\n```\n\nThen install additional dependencies:\n\n```bash\ncd openvla\npip install -r experiments/robot/libero/libero_requirements.txt\n```\n\n## Running Evaluations\n\nExecute evaluation with one of these benchmark-specific commands:\n\n**LIBERO-Spatial:**\n```bash\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\n  --model_family openvla \\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint openvla/openvla-7b-finetuned-libero-spatial \\\n  --task_suite_name libero_spatial \\\n  --center_crop True\n```\n\n**LIBERO-Object:**\n```bash\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\n  --model_family openvla \\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint openvla/openvla-7b-finetuned-libero-object \\\n  --task_suite_name libero_object \\\n  --center_crop True\n```\n\n**LIBERO-Goal:**\n```bash\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\n  --model_family openvla \\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint openvla/openvla-7b-finetuned-libero-goal \\\n  --task_suite_name libero_goal \\\n  --center_crop True\n```\n\n**LIBERO-10 (Long):**\n```bash\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\n  --model_family openvla \\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint openvla/openvla-7b-finetuned-libero-10 \\\n  --task_suite_name libero_10 \\\n  --center_crop True\n```\n\n## Key Configuration Notes\n\n- **Default trials:** 500 total (10 tasks × 50 episodes each); modify via `--num_trials_per_task`\n- **Critical flag:** \"Setting `--center_crop True` is important\" since models were trained with random crop augmentation\n- **GPU requirement:** Testing used NVIDIA A100 GPU\n- **Software versions:** Python 3.10.13, PyTorch 2.2.0, transformers 4.40.1, flash-attn 2.5.5\n- **Optional logging:** Add `--use_wandb True --wandb_project &lt;PROJECT> --wandb_entity &lt;ENTITY>` for Weights & Biases tracking\n- **Random seed:** Adjustable via `--seed` parameter"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"528f7391-9cae-4a61-8339-2f1551cf2f77","uuid":"19e60cc6-bd03-4f53-82f2-b4b158a39eca","timestamp":"2026-05-17T19:12:48.483Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":470483,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# LIBERO Evaluation Setup Instructions\n\n## Clone and Install LIBERO\n\n\"Clone and install the [LIBERO repo](https://github.com/Lifelong-Robot-Learning/LIBERO)\" using these commands:\n\n```bash\ngit clone https://github.com/Lifelong-Robot-Learning/LIBERO.git\ncd LIBERO\npip install -e .\n```\n\nThen install additional dependencies:\n\n```bash\ncd openvla\npip install -r experiments/robot/libero/libero_requirements.txt\n```\n\n## Running Evaluations\n\nExecute evaluation with one of these benchmark-specific commands:\n\n**LIBERO-Spatial:**\n```bash\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\n  --model_family openvla \\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint openvla/openvla-7b-finetuned-libero-spatial \\\n  --task_suite_name libero_spatial \\\n  --center_crop True\n```\n\n**LIBERO-Object:**\n```bash\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\n  --model_family openvla \\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint openvla/openvla-7b-finetuned-libero-object \\\n  --task_suite_name libero_object \\\n  --center_crop True\n```\n\n**LIBERO-Goal:**\n```bash\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\n  --model_family openvla \\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint openvla/openvla-7b-finetuned-libero-goal \\\n  --task_suite_name libero_goal \\\n  --center_crop True\n```\n\n**LIBERO-10 (Long):**\n```bash\npython experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py \\\n  --model_family openvla \\\n  --pretrained_checkpoint openvla/openvla-7b-finetuned-libero-10 \\\n  --task_suite_name libero_10 \\\n  --center_crop True\n```\n\n## Key Configuration Notes\n\n- **Default trials:** 500 total (10 tasks × 50 episodes each); modify via `--num_trials_per_task`\n- **Critical flag:** \"Setting `--center_crop True` is important\" since models were trained with random crop augmentation\n- **GPU requirement:** Testing used NVIDIA A100 GPU\n- **Software versions:** Python 3.10.13, PyTorch 2.2.0, transformers 4.40.1, flash-attn 2.5.5\n- **Optional logging:** Add `--use_wandb True --wandb_project &lt;PROJECT> --wandb_entity &lt;ENTITY>` for Weights & Biases tracking\n- **Random seed:** Adjustable via `--seed` parameter","durationMs":6434,"url":"https://github.com/openvla/openvla/blob/main/README.md"}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EBPDwaCaxP96FF1VGHukB1","type":"tool_result","content":"# Action Tokenizer Overview\n\nThis code extends an LLM tokenizer to handle continuous robot actions by discretizing them into tokens.\n\n## Key Components\n\n**Initialization:**\nThe `ActionTokenizer` creates uniform bins across a continuous action range (default -1 to 1) and reserves \"the last `n_bins` tokens\" for action representation, assuming a BPE-style tokenizer where least-used tokens occupy the vocabulary's end.\n\n**Encoding (Actions → Tokens):**\nThe `__call__` method clips actions to valid bounds, then uses `np.digitize()` to map each value to its bin index. These indices are converted to token IDs by subtracting from `tokenizer.vocab_size`.\n\n**Decoding (Tokens → Actions):**\nThe `decode_token_ids_to_actions()` method reverses this process. It converts token IDs back to bin indices, then retrieves corresponding continuous values from `bin_centers`. A clipping operation handles edge cases where digitization returns indices at bin boundaries.\n\n## Critical Design Note\n\nThe code acknowledges an indexing subtlety: with N bins, digitization produces indices [1, N], but only N-1 bin centers exist. The solution clips bin indices to valid ranges to prevent out-of-bounds access.\n\nThe `vocab_size` property returns `n_bins` (number of action tokens), not the full tokenizer vocabulary."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"528f7391-9cae-4a61-8339-2f1551cf2f77","uuid":"b090249a-ec2e-425f-a246-128516613e2c","timestamp":"2026-05-17T19:16:45.401Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":3758,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# Action Tokenizer Overview\n\nThis code extends an LLM tokenizer to handle continuous robot actions by discretizing them into tokens.\n\n## Key Components\n\n**Initialization:**\nThe `ActionTokenizer` creates uniform bins across a continuous action range (default -1 to 1) and reserves \"the last `n_bins` tokens\" for action representation, assuming a BPE-style tokenizer where least-used tokens occupy the vocabulary's end.\n\n**Encoding (Actions → Tokens):**\nThe `__call__` method clips actions to valid bounds, then uses `np.digitize()` to map each value to its bin index. These indices are converted to token IDs by subtracting from `tokenizer.vocab_size`.\n\n**Decoding (Tokens → Actions):**\nThe `decode_token_ids_to_actions()` method reverses this process. It converts token IDs back to bin indices, then retrieves corresponding continuous values from `bin_centers`. A clipping operation handles edge cases where digitization returns indices at bin boundaries.\n\n## Critical Design Note\n\nThe code acknowledges an indexing subtlety: with N bins, digitization produces indices [1, N], but only N-1 bin centers exist. The solution clips bin indices to valid ranges to prevent out-of-bounds access.\n\nThe `vocab_size` property returns `n_bins` (number of action tokens), not the full tokenizer vocabulary.","durationMs":4070,"url":"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openvla/openvla/main/prismatic/vla/action_tokenizer.py"}}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NgwJzDVJz7gibXuRrTeYkR","type":"tool_result","content":"# OpenVLA Inference Pipeline\n\n## Model Loading & Initialization\n\nThe `OpenVLAServer` class loads the model and processor during initialization:\n\n```python\nself.processor = AutoProcessor.from_pretrained(self.openvla_path, trust_remote_code=True)\nself.vla = AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained(\n    self.openvla_path,\n    attn_implementation=attn_implementation,\n    torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16,\n    low_cpu_mem_usage=True,\n    trust_remote_code=True,\n).to(self.device)\n```\n\nThe model is loaded in `bfloat16` precision and moved to GPU if available.\n\n## predict_action Usage\n\nThe method accepts a payload dictionary containing:\n- `\"image\"`: numpy array (uint8, shape 256×256×3)\n- `\"instruction\"`: string describing the desired action\n- `\"unnorm_key\"` (optional): specifies dataset statistics for denormalizing outputs\n\n## Full Inference Pipeline\n\n1. **Prompt Construction**: A formatted prompt is created based on the instruction and model version\n2. **Preprocessing**: The processor converts the image and prompt into model inputs\n3. **Model Inference**: The VLA model generates an action via `predict_action()`\n4. **Response**: The action is returned as a JSON-serialized numpy array\n\nThe complete flow ensures the model receives properly formatted inputs and returns actionable robot control signals."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"528f7391-9cae-4a61-8339-2f1551cf2f77","uuid":"99e27492-a6e0-4c4e-b91d-6d666695be8c","timestamp":"2026-05-17T19:16:45.675Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":5554,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# AutoProcessor.from_pretrained(self.openvla_path, trust_remote_code=True)\nself.vla = AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained(\n    self.openvla_path,\n    attn_implementation=attn_implementation,\n    torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16,\n    low_cpu_mem_usage=True,\n    trust_remote_code=True,\n).to(self.device)\n```\n\nThe model is loaded in `bfloat16` precision and moved to GPU if available.\n\n## {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FUvPxz7LEVDLpjqhUeUxG2","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\nOpenVLA wrapper for ACR-VLA project.\\\\n\\\\nInterface compatible with SmolVLAWrapper for LIBERO evaluation.\\\\nOpenVLA uses 256-bin discrete tokenization (single-step, no action chunking).\\\\n\\\\nKey differences from SmolVLA:\\\\n  - Action: 256-bin discrete tokens -> 7D continuous (vs flow-matching)\\\\n  - Chunk size: 1 (vs H_eff=5 in SmolVLA)\\\\n  - Hidden dim: 4096 (Llama 2) vs 960 (SmolVLM)\\\\n  - Image: 224x224 via processor (vs 512x512 in SmolVLA)\\\\n  - K samples: multiple forward passes with temperature sampling\\\\n\\\\nFeature extraction: hooks on the last Llama layer capture the hidden state\\\\nfrom the first decode step. In transformers 5.x, the prefill phase populates\\\\nthe KV cache outside the standard forward path, so the first decode step's\\\\nhidden state (conditioned on the full observation via KV cache) serves as the\\\\nobservation feature.\\\\n\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\nimport time\\\\nimport numpy as np\\\\nimport torch\\\\nfrom PIL import Image\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nOPENVLA_HIDDEN_DIM = 4096\\\\nOPENVLA_ACTION_DIM = 7\\\\nOPENVLA_FEATURE_LAYER = 31\\\\n\\\\nUNNORM_KEY_MAP = {\\\\n    \\\\\\\"spatial\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"libero_spatial\\\\\\\",\\\\n    \\\\\\\"object\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"libero_object\\\\\\\",\\\\n    \\\\\\\"goal\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"libero_goal\\\\\\\",\\\\n    \\\\\\\"long\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"libero_10\\\\\\\",\\\\n    \\\\\\\"90\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"libero_90\\\\\\\",\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nclass OpenVLAWrapper:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Frozen OpenVLA for LIBERO evaluation.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n    def __init__(self, {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01W3tUrmzmWdRu28Xi6UUAJE","type":"tool_result","content":"# LIBERO Evaluation Script Overview\n\nThe webpage displays the **complete Python code** for `run_libero_eval.py`, a script that evaluates vision-language action models in LIBERO simulation environments.\n\n## Key Components\n\n**Imports & Setup:**\nThe script imports essential libraries including `draccus` for configuration management, `wandb` for experiment tracking, and custom modules from `experiments.robot` for model loading and environment utilities.\n\n**Configuration:**\nUses a `GenerateConfig` dataclass with parameters for:\n- Model selection (checkpoint path, quantization options)\n- Image processing (\"center crop\" for augmentation-trained models)\n- LIBERO task suite selection (spatial, object, goal variants)\n- Logging preferences (local files and Weights & Biases)\n\n**Environment Creation:**\nThe script calls `get_libero_env(task, cfg.model_family, resolution=256)` to initialize simulation environments. It retrieves initial states via `task_suite.get_task_init_states(task_id)`.\n\n**Evaluation Loop:**\nFor each task and trial:\n1. Resets environment and applies initial states\n2. Waits for object stabilization (`num_steps_wait` parameter)\n3. Preprocesses observations into image and state tensors\n4. Queries the model for actions using `get_action()`\n5. Normalizes gripper actions and executes them\n6. Tracks success when `done==True`\n\n**Success Determination:**\nEpisodes succeed when the environment returns `done=True` after executing predicted actions. The script calculates per-task and overall success rates, logging results locally and optionally to Weights & Biases."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"528f7391-9cae-4a61-8339-2f1551cf2f77","uuid":"26af7181-8f8f-48b7-8d45-3feb2a7adf5d","timestamp":"2026-05-17T19:17:40.479Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":11936,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# LIBERO Evaluation Script Overview\n\nThe webpage displays the **complete Python code** for `run_libero_eval.py`, a script that evaluates vision-language action models in LIBERO simulation environments.\n\n## Key Components\n\n**Imports & Setup:**\nThe script imports essential libraries including `draccus` for configuration management, `wandb` for experiment tracking, and custom modules from `experiments.robot` for model loading and environment utilities.\n\n**Configuration:**\nUses a `GenerateConfig` dataclass with parameters for:\n- Model selection (checkpoint path, quantization options)\n- Image processing (\"center crop\" for augmentation-trained models)\n- LIBERO task suite selection (spatial, object, goal variants)\n- Logging preferences (local files and Weights & Biases)\n\n**Environment Creation:**\nThe script calls `get_libero_env(task, cfg.model_family, resolution=256)` to initialize simulation environments. It retrieves initial states via `task_suite.get_task_init_states(task_id)`.\n\n**Evaluation Loop:**\nFor each task and trial:\n1. Resets environment and applies initial states\n2. Waits for object stabilization (`num_steps_wait` parameter)\n3. Preprocesses observations into image and state tensors\n4. Queries the model for actions using `get_action()`\n5. Normalizes gripper actions and executes them\n6. Tracks success when `done==True`\n\n**Success Determination:**\nEpisodes succeed when the environment returns `done=True` after executing predicted actions. The script calculates per-task and overall success rates, logging results locally and optionally to Weights & Biases.","durationMs":5530,"url":"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openvla/openvla/main/experiments/robot/libero/run_libero_eval.py"}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_017c7NAfunioxJ9rrecA5tJa","type":"tool_result","content":"# Understanding `get_libero_env`\n\nThe `get_libero_env` function initializes a LIBERO simulation environment for policy evaluation. Here's how it works:\n\n## Function Overview\n\n```python\ndef get_libero_env(task, model_family, resolution=256):\n    \"\"\"Initializes and returns the LIBERO environment, along with the task description.\"\"\"\n    task_description = task.language\n    task_bddl_file = os.path.join(get_libero_path(\"bddl_files\"), task.problem_folder, task.bddl_file)\n    env_args = {\"bddl_file_name\": task_bddl_file, \"camera_heights\": resolution, \"camera_widths\": resolution}\n    env = OffScreenRenderEnv(**env_args)\n    env.seed(0)\n    return env, task_description\n```\n\n## How Task Data is Located\n\nThe function locates task data through a **hierarchical file path structure**:\n\n1. **Base Path**: Uses `get_libero_path(\"bddl_files\")` to get the LIBERO installation directory\n2. **Task Folder**: Accesses `task.problem_folder` (a subdirectory within bddl_files)\n3. **BDDL File**: Loads `task.bddl_file` (the actual task specification)\n\nThe task object itself carries metadata—specifically the `language` description and file references needed to construct the full path to the BDDL (Behavior Description Domain Language) file.\n\n## Key Implementation Details\n\n- **Environment Type**: Uses `OffScreenRenderEnv` for headless rendering\n- **Resolution**: Configurable camera resolution (default 256×256)\n- **Seeding**: Sets seed to 0 to ensure reproducible object positions across runs\n- **Returns**: Both the initialized environment and human-readable task description"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"528f7391-9cae-4a61-8339-2f1551cf2f77","uuid":"1699e070-45de-4706-ae8d-06db9013085c","timestamp":"2026-05-17T19:17:49.106Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":3933,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# Understanding `get_libero_env`\n\nThe `get_libero_env` function initializes a LIBERO simulation environment for policy evaluation. Here's how it works:\n\n## Function Overview\n\n```python\ndef get_libero_env(task, model_family, resolution=256):\n    \"\"\"Initializes and returns the LIBERO environment, along with the task description.\"\"\"\n    task_description = task.language\n    task_bddl_file = os.path.join(get_libero_path(\"bddl_files\"), task.problem_folder, task.bddl_file)\n    env_args = {\"bddl_file_name\": task_bddl_file, \"camera_heights\": resolution, \"camera_widths\": resolution}\n    env = OffScreenRenderEnv(**env_args)\n    env.seed(0)\n    return env, task_description\n```\n\n## How Task Data is Located\n\nThe function locates task data through a **hierarchical file path structure**:\n\n1. **Base Path**: Uses `get_libero_path(\"bddl_files\")` to get the LIBERO installation directory\n2. **Task Folder**: Accesses `task.problem_folder` (a subdirectory within bddl_files)\n3. **BDDL File**: Loads `task.bddl_file` (the actual task specification)\n\nThe task object itself carries metadata—specifically the `language` description and file references needed to construct the full path to the BDDL (Behavior Description Domain Language) file.\n\n## Key Implementation Details\n\n- **Environment Type**: Uses `OffScreenRenderEnv` for headless rendering\n- **Resolution**: Configurable camera resolution (default 256×256)\n- **Seeding**: Sets seed to 0 to ensure reproducible object positions across runs\n- **Returns**: Both the initialized environment and human-readable task description","durationMs":5334,"url":"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openvla/openvla/main/experiments/robot/libero/libero_utils.py"}}
